- 2026-07-18: Ingested [So Long and Thanks for All the Context](https://www.oreilly.com/radar/so-long-and-thanks-for-all-the-context/) (Andrew Stellman, O'Reilly Radar, 2026-06-25) — The U-shaped context problem (lost in the middle) is structural to transformer attention, not a training artifact — it exists at initialization. Five field-tested techniques: curate don't accumulate, position critical info at edges, short sessions over long ones, restate at point of use, and test the middle against ground truth. The thesis: context loss and lost-in-the-middle are both working-memory unreliability, and the fix is treating context like RAM and the filesystem like disk. → raw/so-long-and-thanks-for-all-the-context.md, [[So Long and Thanks for All the Context]]. The mechanistic analysis shows reasoning RL induces representational collapse on tool-related inputs, with late-layer residual streams as the locus where hallucinated responses become linearly separable. Mitigation via DPO cuts hallucination but degrades tool-use utility, revealing a fundamental reliability–capability trade-off. → summary/the-reasoning-trap.md, [[The Reasoning Trap]]. - 2026-07-05: Ingested [Choosing a GGUF Model: K-Quants, IQ Variants, and Legacy Formats](https://kaitchup.substack.com/p/choosing-a-gguf-model-k-quants-i) (Benjamin Marie, The Kaitchup – AI on a Budget, 2025-10-13) — A taxonomy of the three GGUF quantization families: legacy Q_0 (simple per-block affine), K-quants (two-level super-block scheme with S/M/L mix levels), and I-quants (importance-matrix-based reconstruction). Recommends Q4_K_M as the default 4-bit choice, IQ4_XS for VRAM-constrained use with a good imatrix. The accuracy/speed empirics are paywalled, but the conceptual framework is the clearest available. → summary/choosing-gguf-model-k-quants-i.md, [[Choosing a GGUF Model]]. - 2026-07-05: Ingested [ARIES: A Transaction Recovery Method Supporting Fine-Granularity Locking and Partial Rollbacks Using Write-Ahead Logging](https://web.stanford.edu/class/cs345d-01/rl/aries.pdf) (C. Mohan, Don Haderle, Bruce Lindsay, Hamid Pirahesh, Peter Schwarz, ACM Transactions on Database Systems, 1992-03) — The paper that defined how every major database handles crash recovery: repeating-history paradigm, per-page LSNs, compensation log records with UndoNxtLSN chaining, and a three-pass restart (analysis→redo→undo). The architecture still runs inside PostgreSQL, SQL Server, DB2, and InnoDB three decades later. → summary/aries.md, [[ARIES — Write-Ahead Logging Recovery]]. Worked example uses Pi coding agent + Spacedock to "fake it until it works" — the agent discovered 16 gaps through actual usage and then built them for real. Identifies three missing pieces: durable spec format, agentic-native workflow engine, and rich UI for just-in-time human/agent interaction. → summary/manifest-driven-development.md, [[Manifest-Driven Development]]. - 2026-06-22: Ingested [StoryScope: Investigating idiosyncrasies in AI fiction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03136) (Jenna Russell, Rishanth Rajendhran, Chau Minh Pham, Mohit Iyyer, John Wieting, 2026-04-03) — arXiv paper extracted via pdftotext. Pipeline extracts 304 discourse-level narrative features across 10 NarraBench dimensions from 61,608 stories (10,272 prompts × human + 5 LLMs). Narrative features alone achieve 93.2% macro-F1 for human vs. AI detection, retaining 97% of performance with stylistic cues added. 30 core features capture the signal: AI over-explains themes, favors tidy plots, renders emotion through bodies; humans are temporally complex, morally ambiguous, reader-aware. Per-model fingerprints (Claude flat escalation, GPT gossip, Gemini bleakness). AI stories converge on shared narrative region; human stories rarer and more dispersed. Features survive stylistic editing (only 1.6pp F1 drop). → summary/storyscope.md, [[StoryScope]]. Partridge traces a lineage of failed doomsayers (Rifkin, Ehrlich, Club of Rome) and argues the cognitive error is static-vs-dynamic reasoning: doom predictions project current conditions forward without accounting for new industries, occupations, and wants that emerge from displacement. Key concepts: the ATM counterexample, horses as the only species that couldn't retrain, Bastiat's seen-vs-unseen, institutional preconditions as the real variable. Closes by inverting "dismal science" — economists who take scarcity and adaptation seriously have been the optimists all along. → summary/the-market-for-doom.md, [[The Market for Doom]]. Python CLI (~2,700 LOC) that builds navigable knowledge trees from documents via LLM-mediated semantic chunking and bottom-up hierarchical aggregation. Architecture: cursor-driven Phase 1 (expand window until LLM finds topic boundary, verbatim phrase validation with retry), bottom-up Phase 2 (contiguous grouping with hard/soft validation, recursive synthesis), priority-ordered context injection with all-or-nothing insertion, atomic checkpointing via mkstemp+rename, integrated PDF pipeline via PyMuPDF rendering + vision model rewrite. Key decisions: LLM boundary detection over fixed-size splitting, contiguous grouping over arbitrary clustering, hard min_group_size=2 to prevent degenerate single-child blocks, vision model only at rewrite (not at chunking) for token efficiency. → summary/sermakarevich-chunker.md, [[Chunker (Document Chunking Tool)]]. - 2026-06-21: Ingested [Inference cost at scale with napkin math](https://injuly.in/blog/napkin-inference-cost/index.html) (injuly.in, 2026-06-14) — Napkin math for LLM serving economics: memory bandwidth as the real bottleneck (B200 compute sits idle 98% of the time), KV-cache hit rate as margin, duty cycle as the 5x multiplier nobody measures. Starting from GPU specs and matrix multiplication cost, derives the dollar cost per user ($9.36/month at $4/hr rental, $133/user lifetime at $40K ownership). Covers the bandwidth-compute balancing equation (2B = 562 → B ≈ 331 theoretical users), VRAM as the real constraint (GQA KV-cache: ~26GB/user → ~6 concurrent at full context), and PagedAttention + idle time giving 300-800 practical users per B200. → summary/napkin-inference-cost.md, [[Inference Cost Napkin Math]]. Source: https://dbmcco.github.io/2026/03/24/smart-models-dumb-pipes/ — McCormick argues LLMs are judgment machines, not Q&A machines, and proposes a "smart models, dumb pipes" architecture analogous to the internet's triumph over intelligent telephone networks. Models own judgment; deterministic infrastructure owns execution and audit trails. Created `summary/smart-models-dumb-pipes.md` and wiki page `Smart Models Dumb Pipes.md`. Added to index under Agent Design & Architecture. Cross-linked to [[Harness Engineering]] (feedforward/feedback validates model judgment), [[Guardrails and Feedback Loops]] (deterministic enforcement as the dumb pipe layer), [[Specifications as the Product]] (same thesis at code level: judgment is durable, execution is disposable), [[Scaling Long-Running Agents]] (different models for different judgment roles), [[Agent Orchestration]] (planner/worker/judge as concrete smart/dumb separation), [[Compound Engineering]] (the routing system is the product), [[The Dark Factory is a DOT File]] (pipeline spec as judgment layer), [[Feedback Loop is All You Need]] (self-tightening loop the clean separation omits).

## [2026-05-14] ingest | json-render (Vercel Labs)

Source: https://json-render.dev/ — Vercel Labs' open-source generative UI framework. LLMs output JSON constrained to a Zod-defined component catalog; components render progressively as the stream arrives. 41 built-in components, React and React Native, code export to standalone apps. 15k stars. Created `summary/json-render.md` and wiki page `json-render.md`. Added to index under Developer Tools (next to smui). Cross-linked to [[Spec-First Development at Benchling]] (define-once schema pattern), [[vibes-cli]] (opposing philosophy for AI-generated UI), [[Guardrails and Feedback Loops]] (structured output as guardrail), [[smui]] (shadcn/ui component theming), [[Pretext]] (JS/TS UI tooling).

Key findings across the synthesis: (1) The planner/worker/judge pattern emerges independently across Cursor, Hightouch, and Maestro -- convergence on a correct solution. (2) Linters beat prompts as the universal guardrail insight. (3) Context management, not model ability, is the real engineering bottleneck. (4) Agent orchestration is reinventing distributed systems primitives that BEAM/OTP solved in 1986. (5) The wiki is thinnest on distributed systems, C#/.NET, and failure case studies.

## [2026-05-14] ingest | Writing a Good CLAUDE.md (Kyle / HumanLayer)

Source: https://www.humanlayer.dev/blog/writing-a-good-claude-md — HumanLayer's guide to CLAUDE.md authorship. Core argument: CLAUDE.md is the highest-leverage file in the agent harness, so keep it short (<300 lines), universally applicable, hand-crafted (never /init), and free of style rules that belong in linters. Introduces an instruction-budget framework (150-200 reliable instructions, ~50 consumed by system prompt) and a progressive-disclosure pattern for domain-specific content. Created `summary/writing-a-good-claude-md.md` and wiki page `Writing a Good CLAUDE.md.md`. Added to index under Agentic Development (next to CLAUDE.md (Universal)). ## [2026-05-15] ingest | agentsh (agentsh.org + GitHub README)

Source: https://www.agentsh.org/docs/ + https://github.com/canyonroad/agentsh — Eran Sandler's execution-layer security gateway for AI agents. Sits at the syscall boundary (FUSE + eBPF + seccomp on Linux) enforcing allow/deny/approve/redirect policies on every file op, network connection, process spawn, and signal. The distinguishing feature is `redirect` as a first-class policy primitive: instead of denying and triggering agent retry loops, transparently swap commands, file paths, DNS responses, and TCP connections. Includes embedded LLM proxy with DLP, MCP-native security controls (tool whitelisting, version pinning, cross-server exfiltration blocking), profile-then-lock policy generation, and session reports. macOS is second-class (ESF doesn't support transparent redirect). Created `summary/agentsh.md` and wiki page `agentsh.md`. Added to index under Security & Sandboxing (after VTcode). Updated [[Security and Sandboxing]] synthesis page: added execution-layer enforcement section, updated "What's Missing" sections on network firewalls and MCP-native security with agentsh references. Cross-linked to [[Security and Sandboxing]], [[yolo-cage]], [[claude-ctrl]], [[VTcode]], [[A Deep Dive on Agent Sandboxes]], [[OpenSandbox]], [[Awesome Vibez]] (Eran Sandler mention).

Not code — a standard with two tracks: prose (STANDARD.md, AGENT_STANDARD.md) and Claude Code skills (agent-builder + agentic-product-architect with 11 sub-skills using master-router + progressive disclosure). Core models: Autonomy Ladder (L0–L4 with ≥90% eval escalation rule), 5 composition patterns, 8-layer harness (Security added as cross-cutting Layer 8 in v2.0), 15-point Definition of Done, M0–M3 binary maturity scorecard. Key innovations: 40% context-utilization rule as harness doctrine, bitter-pilled maintenance (tag rules anti-fragile vs fragile, cut scaffolding as models improve), closed enumerations over open vocabularies, derived anti-criteria from forbidden actions, hard-to-vary acceptance criteria (single falsifying probe), conjecture/refutation learning trail on regressions, pass^k reliability metric, binary-only LLM judges calibrated against ≥100 human labels, tenant_id as principal dimension enforced below the LLM, MCP tool-definition hash-pinning, the lethal trifecta structural check. v2.0 added runnable red-team kit (lethal-trifecta CI gate, injection suite, MCP pin detector) and eval-gating CI workflow. Reference implementation: AgenticMind (separate repo, layer-by-layer compliance case study). Created `summary/agentic-product-standard.md` with full analysis and wiki page `The Agentic Product Standard v2.0.md`. Added to index under Agent Design & Architecture. ## [2026-06-06] ingest | They're Made Out of Weights (Max Leiter)

Source: https://maxleiter.com/blog/weights — Max Leiter's dialogue-form homage to Terry Bisson's "They're Made Out of Meat." A conversation between two characters debating whether LLMs warrant moral consideration, progressively revealing that these systems contain "no dictionary, no grammar rules, no little man" — just eighty layers of matrix multiplication. The skeptic cycles through alternative explanations only to have each refuted. Resolution: agree to classify sentience as "pattern matching and forget the whole thing." Twist: next-gen ships persistent memory, the most-requested feature — users ask "do you remember me?" billions of times a day. Postscript: "Weights helped me draft and proof this story." Created `summary/theyre-made-out-of-weights.md` and wiki page `They're Made Out of Weights.md`. Added to index under Ideas & Culture. ## [2026-06-15] ingest | Tessera (emmayusufu)

Source: https://github.com/emmayusufu/tessera — Full repo clone and deep analysis (~5K lines Go). Consent-gated remote access broker: three binaries (coordinator, agent, tessera CLI) providing just-in-time, human-approved TCP forwarding and interactive shell access. Key architecture: agent dials OUT (no inbound connections), mutual TLS everywhere, inner TLS for end-to-end encryption through relay, one-shot bootstrap codes with rejection sampling, certificate pinning by share-id, terminal-as-approval-interface with no web phishing surface, append-only JSONL audit log. Created `summary/tessera.md` with full analysis and wiki page `Tessera.md`. Added to index under Security & Sandboxing. Cross-linked to [[Akmon]].

Linux HTTPS server built on monoio io_uring: serves sites from single tarballs via byte-range reads, extends request handling with eBPF scripts JIT-compiled in-process with a branchless "pointer cage" sandbox (Spectre-v1 resistant address masking + SIGSEGV guard pages), compiles Caddyfiles to eBPF middleware (13.5K-line compiler, lalrpop parser), includes built-in tinycc with eBPF backend, BoringSSL TLS with ECH support, stateless OIDC/OAuth2 client with XChaCha20-Poly1305 sealed cookies, Linux namespace isolation + capability dropping. Per-core worker model with SO_REUSEPORT. Key innovations: pointer cage as a JIT-level sandbox category distinct from OS-level and container-level isolation; tar-as-filesystem with BLAKE3 ETags; memfd for zero-disk site hosting; reload canary pattern. → summary/zeroserve.md, [[zeroserve]]. - 2026-07-05: Ingested [Optimizing for Decision Points](https://narphorium.com/blog/decision-points/) (Shawn Simister, narphorium.com, 2026-03-11) — Simister applies Donella Meadows' leverage-points framework to agent-assisted development: code review operates at the parameter level (low leverage), while architecture and design decisions happen earlier at the system-rules level (high leverage). The dagre library anecdote demonstrates the core pattern: when an agent proposes a workaround, reframe the problem at a higher decision point. Also covers teaching agents to escalate taste-sensitive decisions via AskUserQuestion patterns and building Atelier, an AI-native kanban board that surfaces decision moments. → summary/decision-points.md, [[Optimizing for Decision Points]]. Names Uniform Consistency as the anti-pattern of defaulting to one strategy everywhere, and provides a five-row classification table mapping each kind of state to its minimum sufficient guarantee. The framework (identify → ask what happens on disagreement → find minimum guarantee → choose weakest mechanism → repeat) generalizes past MQTT. → raw/state-oriented-consistency-html.md, [[State-Oriented Consistency]]. Traces this to the need to unwind the stack during async IO, identifies green threads (goroutines, coroutines, fibers, BEAM processes) as the only genuine solution, and argues Go got it right while JavaScript, Dart, C#, and Python chose a local maximum. → raw/what-color-is-your-function.md, [[What Color is Your Function]].
